| `perilous-forays-private-basic-land-choice-missing` | RAV card-fidelity / hidden-zone decision gap — Perilous Forays had an ordinary stack activation with an explicit creature-sacrifice cost, but resolved its basic-land search by library order instead of giving its controller the required private choose-or-decline boundary. | Fixed; red `e8f49812`, green `9892e44e` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test perilous_forays_policy_search_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` initially recorded `AbilityManaPaid → SacrificedAsAbilityCost → AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Forest, Battlefield) → LibrarySearchResolved → LibraryShuffled → AbilityResolved`, with `pending_decision=None`; the controller could not choose the legal Plains or decline. Green replaces the deterministic selector with the existing one-effect private policy search and promotes the complete card. The reviewed non-first trace is `DecisionOpened(LibrarySearch, private, player 0) → DecisionCompleted → CardMoved(Plains, BattlefieldTapped) → LibrarySearchResolved → LibraryShuffled → AbilityResolved`; Forest remains hidden in the library, the opponent sees no pending choice, and final invariants hold. Coverage becomes 246 full / 12 partial / 33 catalog-only unique names (261 / 12 / 33 printings). |
| `global-damage-replacement-choice-missing` | Engine replacement/state-machine defect — an all-creature-and-player damage instruction resolves each packet through the legacy deterministic direct-damage path. A creature simultaneously protected by a shield and bounded redirection receives whichever replacement the implementation visits first, instead of its controller receiving the required replacement-order decision. | Fixed; red `04372049`, green `c67fe60d` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test global_damage_replacement_choice_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Red trace ends `DamageDealtToPermanent(redirector,2) → DamageRedirected(target→opponent,2) → DamageDealtToPlayer(opponent,2) → SpellResolved`, with `stack=[]` and `pending=None`; it fails at `the protected global-damage packet must open a replacement decision`. Green snapshots all current creature/player packets, retains the unresolved global instruction, and gives the protected creature's controller the id-bearing public choice. Its event trace is `DamageDealtToPermanent(redirector,2) → DecisionOpened(Replacement) → DamageReplacementApplied(TargetedShield) → DamagePrevented(target,2) → DecisionCompleted → DamageDealtToPlayer(player 0,2) → DamageDealtToPlayer(player 1,2) → SpellResolved`; no redirect fires, the stack resolves exactly once, and the invariant audit passes. Scope remains the represented `DealDamageToEachCreatureAndPlayer` instruction; other multi-recipient effect kinds are distinct future coverage. |


| `life-from-the-loam-public-land-selection-missing` | RAV card-fidelity / public-zone decision gap — Life from the Loam resolved its "up to three" graveyard-land return by stable graveyard order, rather than suspending for the casting controller to select zero through three legal land cards. | Fixed; red `1464fc3c`, green `a27ae1b9` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test life_from_the_loam_policy_choice_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` initially moved `ObjectId(2)`, `ObjectId(3)`, and `ObjectId(4)` directly to hand after the second priority pass, then recorded `SpellResolved`; `GameView.pending_decision` was `None`. Green adds an invariant-audited public `PublicGraveyardLandReturn` decision with an exact controller/stack/source-incarnation boundary and zero-through-three unique legal land selections. Its focused trace is `SpellCast → PriorityPassed ×2 → DecisionOpened(PublicGraveyardLandReturn, player 0, 0..3) → DecisionCompleted → CardMoved(selected Forest) → CardMoved(selected Island) → SpellResolved → source CardMoved(Graveyard)`. The non-prefix selection leaves Mountain in the graveyard; an opponent answer is rejected atomically, and the final state passes `validate_invariants()`. Coverage becomes 245 full / 13 partial / 33 catalog-only unique names (260 / 13 / 33 printings). |
| `controlled-counter-replacement-uses-owner` | Engine layer-two/replacement defect — the fallback counter-placement path used a permanent object's owner/base controller as the affected player. A stolen permanent therefore ignored counter multipliers controlled by its live controller. | **Fixed; red `99439112`, green `ac735ca9`** | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test controlled_counter_replacement_controller_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Player 2 activates a `{0}` ability to put one charge counter on player 0's artifact after player 2's source gave player 1 control of it; player 1 controls the only 2× counter multiplier. Red fails `left: Some(1), right: Some(2)` with no `ReplacementEffectApplied` for player 1. The repair makes the direct single-replacement fallback use `controller_of(target)`, matching the existing multi-choice stack suspension path. Green has two charge counters and `ReplacementEffectApplied { affected_player: PlayerId(1), 1 → 2 }`; standard counter, multi-instruction quantity, control-change, and invariant checks remain green. |

| `controlled-leaves-trigger-uses-owner` | Engine control/trigger lifecycle defect — generic `AnotherCreatureLeavesBattlefield` capture recorded a source object's owner/base controller instead of its live layer-two controller. A stolen observer's trigger therefore stacked under the wrong player. The adjacent generic dies capture had the same owner-field defect. | **Fixed; red `ef9a4e85` / `a776b2e1`, green `61a2eaca`** | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test controlled_leave_trigger_controller_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. A player-0 observer is controlled by player 1 through player 2's live control effect; player 2 bounces another player-0 creature. Red log ends `TriggeredAbilityStacked { controller: PlayerId(0), source: observer, ability: "another-creature-left" }`, and the test fails because the legal controller is player 1. The expanded probe also gives another player-0 creature -1 toughness and reports the same incorrect `controller: PlayerId(0)` for `another-creature-died`. The repair captures `controller_of(source)` while the source is still on the battlefield in both generic observer paths. Green contracts prove each event stacks for player 1 and retains correct source/incarnation provenance; generic leaves/dies, control-change, engine-library, format, and strict Clippy checks pass. |

| `terminal-player-loss-stacks-trigger` | Engine terminal-state defect — a player-loss SBA that ended a two-player game still flushed pending battlefield-leave triggers before recording `GameEnded`. The result left a triggered ability on the stack after the final terminal receipt, despite there being no further priority or resolution. | **Fixed; red `c2c30ac7`, green `f1cf3e7e`** | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test terminal_player_loss_trigger_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. The terminal owner-departure fixture logged `PlayerLost(player 1) → ObjectLeftGame(creature) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(observer) → GameEnded(winner 0)`, then failed because the stack was nonempty. The repair ends a terminal SBA fixed point before APNAP trigger placement: newly observed trigger events and placements are discarded, while any pre-existing stack provenance remains frozen only for event-log audit. Green trace ends `PlayerLost(player 1) → ObjectLeftGame(creature) → GameEnded(winner 0)` with no trigger receipt or stack object; `GameEnded` remains the unique final receipt. |

| `owner-departure-misses-leaves-battlefield-trigger` | Engine CR 800.4a / trigger lifecycle defect — when an owned creature left the game with its owner, departure cleanup removed the object directly without capturing surviving `AnotherCreatureLeavesBattlefield` observers. This made a player-loss battlefield departure invisible to live leave-the-battlefield triggers. | **Fixed; red `c8ef2662`, green `76d09869`** | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test owner_departure_leaves_trigger_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. The three-player fixture has player 0's creature observe another creature leaving, then eliminates player 1 while its creature is on the battlefield. Red log is only `PlayerLost(player 1) → ObjectLeftGame(creature)`, and the test fails because no `TriggeredAbilityStacked { source: observer, ability: "another-creature-left" }` appears. The repair captures surviving observers at last-known battlefield state before direct owner-object removal, without turning the departure into a graveyard move or dies event. Green trace is `PlayerLost(player 1) → ObjectLeftGame(creature) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(observer) → PriorityPassed ×2 → CounterPlaced(observer) → AbilityResolved`; the observer gains exactly one counter and invariants hold. |
| `departing-controller-token-exile` | Engine CR 800.4a / token lifecycle defect — a token owned by one player but controlled by another was treated as a non-owned permanent when its controller left the game, so elimination cleanup moved it to its owner's exile zone instead of making it cease to exist. This violated the engine's own invariant that tokens exist only on the battlefield and left an invalid public zone object behind. | **Fixed; red `2b3111f7`, green `53734cb6`** | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test eliminated_token_control_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. The three-player fixture resolves a Saproling for player 0, has player 2's live source grant player 1 control, then eliminates player 1 at an SBA boundary. Red log: `PlayerLost(player 1) → CardMoved(token, Exile) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → ContinuousEffectExpired`; the test failed because `game.object(token)` still existed rather than emitting `TokenCeasedToExist`. The narrow repair gives CR 800.4a a token-specific departure path: it preserves leaves-the-battlefield observation and combat cleanup, emits `TokenCeasedToExist`, expires effects, and performs no owner-zone move, incarnation advance, or dies-only trigger dispatch. Green trace: `PlayerLost(player 1) → TokenCeasedToExist(token) → ContinuousEffectExpired`; the token is absent, no `CardMoved { to: Exile }` names it, and `validate_invariants()` passes. |

| `trigger-order-decision-priority-handoff` | Engine priority/trigger state-machine defect — completing a no-priority APNAP trigger-order decision leaves priority with the ordering controller instead of restoring the player who held priority before trigger placement. | Fixed; red `5c6c4e76`, green `d44b791c` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test trigger_order_priority_handoff_red -- --nocapture`. The synthetic fixture has player zero play a land while player one owns two simultaneous landfall triggers. After `DecisionCompleted → TriggeredAbilityOrderChosen → TriggeredAbilityStacked ×2`, it reports `priority=PlayerId(1)` and fails `left: PlayerId(1), right: PlayerId(0)`. The repair retains the original holder through APNAP order/target decision chains and restores it only after the full batch stacks. Green trace ends with the same ordered two trigger receipts and `priority=PlayerId(0)`; its private provenance invariant rejects a missing, departed, or mismatched saved holder. |

| `cast-opponent-trigger-order-priority-reset` | Engine priority/trigger state-machine defect — casting a noncreature spell can synchronously open an opponent-owned APNAP trigger-order decision, but the cast tail overwrites the required decision holder with the caster, so invariant validation rolls back the otherwise legal cast. | Fixed; red `ed760f67`, green `6dba9773` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test cast_opponent_trigger_order_priority_red -- --nocapture`. The synthetic fixture gives player one two `FirstNoncreatureSpellCastEachTurn` triggers, then player zero casts a legal instant. The result is `Err(IllegalAction("pending decision violates its identity, cardinality, or no-priority boundary"))` with `trace=[]` after rollback. The repair preserves the decision holder installed by APNAP trigger placement. Green trace: `SpellCast → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → FirstNoncreatureSpellCastThisTurn → DecisionOpened(TriggeredAbilityOrder, player 1)`, after which player one orders the group, both trigger objects stack above the original spell, and invariants hold. |

| `triggered-damage-replacement-choice-missing` | Engine replacement/trigger state-machine defect — a targeted triggered ability that contains a middle `DealDamage` instruction silently bypasses the affected player's concurrent prevention/redirection ordering decision. The existing continuation explicitly recognizes spells only, so a legal trigger falls through the deterministic direct-damage resolver. | Fixed; red `61a8b232`, green `ef7ec093` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test triggered_damage_replacement_choice_red -- --nocapture`. A synthetic attacking trigger resolves `GainLife(1) → DealDamage(2) → GainLife(2)` against a creature with both a two-point shield and two-point redirection. Red records `LifeGained → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer → LifeGained → AbilityResolved`, with `stack=[]` and `pending=None`, then fails because no `DecisionKind::Replacement` opens. Green removes the source-category exclusion while retaining exact stack-id/effect-index/target-occurrence provenance and invariants. The focused trace pauses after the prefix at `DecisionOpened(Replacement)`, then records `DamageReplacementApplied(Redirect) → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer → DecisionCompleted → LifeGained(2) → AbilityResolved`; the source ability and its suffix resolve exactly once under a valid invariant audit. |

| `multi-instruction-damage-replacement-choice-missing` | Engine replacement/stack state-machine defect — competing damage prevention and redirection effects exposed an affected-player `DecisionKind::Replacement` boundary only when the resolving spell contained one `DealDamage` instruction. A legal prefix/damage/suffix spell silently used the direct resolver's deterministic redirection path, skipping the affected player's ordering choice. | Fixed; red `e02d9840`, green `38cc1421` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multi_instruction_damage_replacement_red -- --nocapture`. With a two-point shield and a two-point redirection both applicable, the synthetic `GainLife(1) → DealDamage(2) → GainLife(2)` spell ended with `stack=[]`, `pending=None`, then `DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer` before the suffix. The test failed because the middle damage did not open a generic replacement decision. The green continuation captures the live stack id, exact effect index, and exact target occurrence. Its focused trace now pauses after `LifeGained(1)` at `DecisionOpened(Replacement)`, records `DamageReplacementApplied(Redirect) → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer → DecisionCompleted`, then resolves only the suffix `LifeGained(2) → SpellResolved`; both the paused and final game states pass `validate_invariants()`. Scope remains targeted direct-damage instant/sorcery instructions; activated-ability damage instructions, optional replacements, and the full replacement-event algebra remain open. |

| `grozoth-optional-etb-multi-search-and-stack-transmute-missing` | RAV card-fidelity gap — Grozoth remained a bounded Defender/immediate-Transmute chassis even though the engine now has optional trigger, private multi-card library-search, and stack-backed Transmute substrates. Its optional ETB multi-search is absent, and its definition still claims the retired immediate helper. | Fixed; red `b663849e`, green `36be9378` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test grozoth_full_fidelity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` initially failed before mutation because no `etb-search-mana-value-nine` binding existed. Green registers a target-free optional ETB trigger with a controller-private, policy-submitted zero-or-more mana-value-nine search, public reveal, and shuffle; it promotes Transmute to the ordinary stack-backed private search. The reviewed accept trace is `TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → DecisionOpened(LibrarySearch, private) → DecisionCompleted → CardRevealed/CardMoved ×2 → LibrarySearchBatchResolved → LibraryShuffled → AbilityResolved`; decline emits only `AbilityResolved` with no candidate projection. Both branches and stack-backed Transmute pass `validate_invariants()`. The retained public immediate-helper scenario is explicitly documented as historical compatibility evidence, not policy fidelity. |

| `optional-trigger-decline-opens-deferred-search` | Engine optional-trigger state-machine defect — declining an optional triggered ability whose first effect requires a private resolution-time decision does not skip the ability. The resolver opens that deferred decision before consuming the declined optional result, exposing library candidates and leaving the stack nonterminal. | Fixed; red `96baa7ef`, green `ac56d696` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test optional_trigger_decline_search_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` initially reported `pending=Some(PendingDecisionView { kind: LibrarySearch, ... })` after `pay: false`. Green consumes a decline before every effect-specific suspension, then emits only the terminal `AbilityResolved` receipt; the matching card remains in the library, no private candidate projection or `DecisionOpened(LibrarySearch)` occurs, and `validate_invariants()` succeeds. |

| `mausoleum-turnkey-optional-etb-choice-missing` | RAV card-fidelity gap — Mausoleum Turnkey's intervening-condition ETB trigger was executable but mandatory, and its definition still advertised deterministic target selection. The controller could not decline the return through the public policy boundary. | Fixed; red `90259115`, green `968153b8` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test mausoleum_turnkey_full_fidelity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed 0/1 at `the ETB return must wait for its controller's explicit may decision` because the binding had `optional: false`. Green retains the current public, id-bearing target decision before stacking; after ordinary priority passes the controller alone accepts or declines the zero-cost may trigger. The reviewed accept trace selects the second graveyard creature and records `DecisionOpened/Completed(TriggeredAbilityTargets) → TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(selected, Hand) → AbilityResolved`; the decline trace preserves both creature cards in the graveyard and emits only the terminal ability receipt. Both boundaries pass the invariant audit, and the full-fidelity promotion updates the checked public scenario digest. |
| `multi-instruction-target-player-mana-choice-missing` | Engine stack/choice state-machine defect — a target player's resolution-time color choice was valid only when it was the sole effect of a spell or activated ability. A legal multi-instruction ability could not even be represented, blocking any prefix/choice/suffix stack lifecycle and forcing future card modules toward special-case effects. | Fixed; red `0444019a`, green `27fc4328` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multi_instruction_target_player_mana_color_red -- --nocapture`. The synthetic `GainLife(1) → AddOneManaOfTargetPlayersChosenColor → GainLife(2)` targeted activated ability failed at construction with `IllegalAction("a target-player mana choice must be the only effect on an ability")`. Green retains the immutable complete stack effect list and target-occurrence provenance, records an invariant-audited cursor for the exact mana instruction, and holds a selected colored output only as short-lived typed materialization. The event trace is `AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → LifeGained(1) → DecisionOpened(TargetPlayerManaColor, player 1) → DecisionCompleted → ManaAdded(player 1, Green) → LifeGained(2) → AbilityResolved`. The regression proves player 0 cannot answer player 1's choice, validates a matching spell definition as well as the activated ability, and verifies the ordinary one-effect spell contract remains green. |

| `multi-instruction-quantity-replacement-choice-missing` | Engine replacement state-machine defect — concurrent token/counter quantity replacements exposed an affected-player ordering decision only when the resolving stack item contained exactly one matching instruction. A valid multi-instruction spell instead resolved every instruction and applied multipliers in stable object-id order, silently removing the affected player's CR 616 choice. | Fixed; red `08057ea8`, green `a545d980` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multi_instruction_quantity_replacement_red -- --nocapture`. The red `CreateToken(1) → GainLife(1)` fixture resolved with `stack=[]`, `pending=None`, life 21, six created tokens, and two `ReplacementEffectApplied` receipts in object-id order. Green keeps the immutable full stack instruction/target provenance, adds an invariant-audited private cursor for the exact pending quantity instruction, and resumes only the suffix after selection. The stronger green trace is `LifeGained(1) → DecisionOpened(Replacement) → ReplacementEffectApplied(1→3) → DecisionCompleted → ReplacementEffectApplied(3→6) → TokenCreated ×6 → LifeGained(2) → SpellResolved → CardMoved(Graveyard)`. It proves a middle instruction pauses without priority after its prefix and completes its suffix exactly once. |

| `multi-instruction-targeted-discard-choice-missing` | Engine stack/private-information state-machine defect — the recipient-private targeted-discard boundary recognizes only a one-instruction stack item. A valid multi-instruction spell resolves an earlier life-gain instruction, then deterministically discards the oldest hidden recipient hand card, then resolves its suffix instead of suspending for the recipient's policy choice. | Fixed; red `19645016`, green `9570a28a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multi_instruction_targeted_discard_red -- --nocapture`. To avoid contention with unrelated long-running Cargo jobs, the already-built test binary was also run with a five-second process bound. The synthetic `GainLife(1) → DiscardTargetPlayer(1) → GainLife(2)` spell ended with `stack=[]`, `pending=None`, recipient hand `[ObjectId(3)]`, and `CardDiscarded { player: PlayerId(1), card: ObjectId(2) }`, then failed because no private `ConditionalPrivateDiscard` decision existed. Green preserves immutable full-effect and target-occurrence provenance in an invariant-audited cursor, pauses at the exact middle discard, and reveals only player 1's hand options. Its trace is `LifeGained(1) → DecisionOpened(private, player 1) → DecisionCompleted → CardDiscarded(selected ObjectId(3)) → CardMoved(Graveyard) → LifeGained(2) → SpellResolved → CardMoved(spell, Graveyard)`. The regression proves the caster cannot inspect the private prompt, player 1 selects the second rather than deterministic first card, the prefix/suffix execute once, and invariants hold both while paused and after completion. |

| `optional-trigger-quantity-resume-reopens-payment` | Engine stack/trigger state-machine defect — an optional trigger accepted at its resolution boundary can pause for a later middle-instruction quantity replacement. Resuming that suffix re-enters the generic optional-trigger prompt path, even though the policy already accepted the trigger. The stale cursor then violates its own no-priority continuation invariant and rolls back the replacement choice. | Fixed; red `93568db6`, green `3e2ce8ea` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test optional_trigger_quantity_resume_red -- --nocapture`. The synthetic `BeginningOfUpkeep` trigger is accepted at its zero-mana optional decision, then resolves `GainLife(1) → CreateToken(1) → GainLife(2)` through concurrent 2×/3× token multipliers. Submitting the first multiplier fails atomically with `IllegalAction("resumable stack instruction cursor escaped its quantity decision")`; that cursor was left live while the resumed resolver reopened the already-consumed optional-trigger choice. Green admits the optional prompt and charges its mana cost only at cursor zero. A stronger `{1}`-mana trace is `AbilityManaPaid → LifeGained(1) → DecisionOpened(Replacement) → ReplacementEffectApplied(1→3) → DecisionCompleted → ReplacementEffectApplied(3→6) → TokenCreated ×6 → LifeGained(2) → AbilityResolved`; it proves the accepted payment is neither reopened nor charged twice, and the final state has no stack or decision boundary. |

| `trigger-breadth-private-discard-contract-drift` | Engine integration-test contract drift — the opponent-graveyard trigger regression still attempted to pass priority immediately after a targeted discard spell resolved, despite the later recipient-private discard substrate correctly opening a mandatory decision for the target player. This is not a production-rule defect: the test omitted the policy action required to move the selected hand card to its owner's graveyard. | Fixed; red `84d3acc2`, green `3a9d3678` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test trigger_breadth_red -- --nocapture`. `opponent_graveyard_trigger_observes_a_discard_from_hand` failed at `pass pending trigger: IllegalAction("the pending decision must resolve before priority actions")` on line 226. The test-only repair asserts the private `ConditionalPrivateDiscard` prompt visible to player 1, submits the sole legal hand card through `PolicyAction::SubmitDecision`, then passes priority to resolve the already-correct opponent-graveyard trigger. The green trace is `DecisionOpened → DecisionCompleted → CardDiscarded → CardMoved(Graveyard) → SpellResolved → TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → CounterPlaced → AbilityResolved`, preserving the target player's hidden hand choice and the observer's normal trigger lifecycle. |

| `blood-funnel-targeted-spell-fixture-drift` | RAV integration-test contract drift — Blood Funnel's no-creature branch used Dryad's Caress as a target-free noncreature spell, but its promoted graveyard-return implementation now correctly requires a creature-card target. The fixture therefore fails cast target-shape validation before it can exercise the Blood Funnel trigger. This is not an engine target-legality defect. | Fixed; red `b8c7160b`, green `eb113a96` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test blood_funnel_full_fidelity -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. `noncreature_cast_without_a_creature_is_countered_by_its_own_trigger` failed at `spell and trigger enter stack: IllegalAction("the supplied targets do not match the spell's target occurrences")`. The test-only repair uses target-free Scatter the Seeds, whose `{3}{G}{G}` cost becomes exactly three green mana under Blood Funnel's two-generic reduction. Green trace is `SpellCast → TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → SpellCountered → CardMoved(Graveyard) → AbilityResolved`; it proves the target-free fixture reaches the intended no-creature counter branch without weakening target-shape validation. |

| `rav-long-event-transcript-clippy-drift` | RAV integration-test style drift — two recent public event-log transcript tests exceed the strict 100-line Clippy threshold, making all-target RAV/workspace Clippy fail despite the production code being warning-free. This is test-style debt, not a production warning or engine defect. | Fixed; red `d25b5b64`, green `a3841b72` | Exact red command: `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-rav --all-targets -- -D warnings` from `varieties/magic`. It rejected `golgari_grave_troll_entry_regeneration_red.rs:28` (126 lines) and `empty_the_catacombs_policy_choice_red.rs:9` (105 lines) under `clippy::too_many_lines`. Green adds documented scoped annotations on only those two transcript functions; it does not relax workspace or production lint policy. |

| `rav-long-event-transcript-clippy-second-drift` | RAV integration-test style drift — after the first two test-local transcript annotations, strict all-target Clippy exposed two older public scenario transcripts over the same 100-line threshold. This is again test-only style debt, not a production warning or engine defect. | Fixed; red `38cf4f5b`, green `2f00108b` | Exact red command: `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-rav --all-targets -- -D warnings` from `varieties/magic`, after `a3841b72`. | `rav-long-event-transcript-clippy-third-drift` | RAV integration-test style drift — after both prior scoped transcript pairs, strict all-target Clippy exposed one remaining oversized public event-log transcript. | `rav-long-event-transcript-clippy-remaining-drift` | RAV integration-test style drift — a source-level audit of public `#[test]` functions found a final finite group of transcript contracts over Clippy's 100-line threshold. | `overgrown-tomb-test-bool-comparison-clippy-drift` | RAV integration-test style drift — after transcript lint cleanup, strict Clippy reached a boolean comparison in the Overgrown Tomb test that can be stated as a direct negation. This is test-expression debt, not a production warning or engine defect. | Fixed; red `c0f6b26f`, green `7f392599` | Exact red command: `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-rav --all-targets -- -D warnings` from `varieties/magic`, after the transcript annotations. | `public-continuous-effect-installation-not-atomic` | Engine continuous-effect transaction defect — an invalid public `AddDamageShield(-1)` installation mutates the target, installs an effect, and appends `ContinuousEffectCreated` before the final invariant audit returns an error. | Fixed; red `01c75063`, green `c7ff7576` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test continuous_effect_install_atomicity_red -- --nocapture`. In a started two-player game, the public `add_continuous_effect` request with a negative end-of-turn shield returned `Err(IllegalAction("object has impossible turn metadata, damage/shield, or counters"))` only after target `damage_shield` became `-1`, a live effect remained, and the canonical log gained `ContinuousEffectCreated`. Green preflights the shield amount and wraps the entire public installation in the rollback transaction. The fixed trace is `Err(IllegalAction("damage shield effect requires a positive amount")); shield=Ok(0); effects=[]`, with only the preexisting step receipts. The live-effect invariant independently rejects a nonpositive shield amount. |

| `static-continuous-global-layer-order` | Engine continuous-layer defect — static characteristics changes are applied before timestamped layer-four/five changes, so a static color/type-dependent layer-six or layer-seven rule can inspect stale characteristics. | Fixed; red `b42871cc`, green `dc82760d` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test static_continuous_layer_order_red -- --nocapture`. A red 2/2 shares no color with the controller's green creature library top. A live layer-five `ReplaceColorsWith(Green)` makes it green until end of turn; red recorded `Characteristics { colors: {Green}, power: Some(2), toughness: Some(2) }`, failing `left: (Some(2), Some(2))`, `right: (Some(3), Some(3))`. Green phases evaluation as timestamped type/color, static layer-six/seven bindings, then timestamped ability/P/T effects. The same `ContinuousEffectCreated { layer: Color }` trace now derives green `3/3`, and the engine preserves timestamp order inside each timestamped phase. |

| `plus-minus-counter-pair-sba-missing` | Engine SBA defect — persistent `+1/+1` and `-1/-1` counters both remain on a creature, so derived power/toughness looks correct while the game state violates CR 704.5q and later counter-dependent rules can read the wrong quantities. | Fixed; red `82ffb077`, green `99011d6a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test counter_pair_sba_red -- --nocapture`. Two zero-cost spells place two `+1/+1` counters then one `-1/-1` counter on a 2/2. Red retained `+1/+1: 2` and `-1/-1: 1`, failing `left: Some(2)`, `right: Some(1)`. Green cancels the minimum pair count before creature SBAs, so the exact trace adds `CounterPairsRemovedByStateBasedAction { card: ObjectId(1), amount: 1 }` after the second spell's terminal movement. The stable map is one `+1/+1` and no `-1/-1`; the live invariant rejects a persisted opposing pair, the receipt invariant rejects a nonpositive source-free cancellation, and measured event accounting requires each visible pair removal to have enough visible placements. |

| `etb-trigger-sba-incarnation-provenance` | Engine stack/SBA provenance defect — a permanent that entered, then immediately left to a state-based action, could have its ETB trigger stacked with the later graveyard incarnation rather than the battlefield object that actually triggered. | Fixed; red `5389408e`, green `9c383594` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test etb_trigger_sba_incarnation_red -- --nocapture`. Red recorded `CardMoved(Battlefield) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced(3) → StateBasedAction → CardMoved(Graveyard) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced(4) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(source_incarnation: 4)`, then failed `left: 4`, `right: 3`. The repair splits entry-trigger capture from trigger placement: self ETB and affected entry observers retain live battlefield provenance before SBAs, while the shared pending-trigger pipeline still places them only after the SBA fixed point. Green records the identical entry/SBA order followed by `TriggeredAbilityStacked(source_incarnation: 3)`. The invariant requires every self-ETB stack receipt to name the incarnation immediately preceded by that source's `CardMoved(Battlefield)` receipt. |
| `stoneshaker-shaman-active-end-step-untapped-land-sacrifice-missing` | RAV Red/core end-step-trigger gap — the engine could make the active player sacrifice a creature at each upkeep, but could not bind the equivalent target-free choice lifecycle to an untapped land at each end step. Stoneshaker Shaman was catalog-only. | Fixed; red `c6b67c2e`, green `3354c849` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test stoneshaker_shaman_red -- --nocapture` failed 0/1 at `Stoneshaker Shaman definition exists`. The green path adds `BeginningOfAnyEndStep`, captures the active player before priority, materializes an untapped-land-only selection effect, and rejects wrong-player, tapped, stale, and opponent-controlled submissions atomically. Its trace reaches `StepBegan(End) → TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → DecisionOpened → DecisionCompleted → SacrificedByEffect → CardMoved(Graveyard) → AbilityResolved`; on the following player's end step, that player—not the source controller—owns the decision and sacrifice. No legal untapped land now resolves directly without a fabricated zero-option prompt. Public scenario: `rav_stoneshaker_shaman_end_step_untapped_land_sacrifice` (`fnv1a64:65e99e0445036d4c`). |

| `stack-draw-loss-sba-timing` | Engine stack/SBA defect — an empty-library draw inside a resolving spell immediately eliminated its controller, removing the source before the rest of that spell and its terminal lifecycle could complete. | Fixed; red `e2997a1f`, green `7e8a90a9` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_draw_loss_sba_timing_red -- --nocapture`. The red test could not find `SpellResolved`: the first instruction immediately recorded `PlayerLost`/`ObjectLeftGame`. The repair keeps a private exact-player empty-library marker through the remaining instructions and terminal source-zone receipt, blocks ordinary priority while it exists, then consumes it in `check_state_based_actions`. Exact green trace: `LifeGained(1) → SpellResolved → CardMoved(Graveyard) → PlayerLost(empty library) → ObjectLeftGame → GameEnded`; the legacy one-effect spell contract records the same terminal ordering. The invariant rejects a marker that escapes its resolving stack object/no-priority continuation or SBA boundary. |

| `guild-bounce-land-full-fidelity-classification-gap` | RAV land/card-fidelity gap — Boros Garrison, Dimir Aqueduct, Golgari Rot Farm, and Selesnya Sanctuary already had typed tapped-entry, controller-land return, and fixed two-mana bundle behavior, but remained bounded and absent from the positive manifest. | Fixed; red `4e695dba`, green `d32a3849` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test guild_bounce_lands_full_fidelity_red -- --nocapture` failed 4/4 only on missing `full-rules-fidelity` markers and positive-manifest membership. | `guild-bounce-land-prebegin-fixture-drift` | RAV test-fixture drift — legacy bounce-land event tests called `play_land` before `begin_game`, after which their historical direct-stack expectation no longer represented a live-game target-decision transition. | Resolved as fixture correction; green `d32a3849` | Running `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test guild_bounce_lands_red -- --nocapture` on the pre-green state failed three cases with an empty stack (`left: 0`, `right: 1`) or an index-out-of-bounds access. | `combat-prevention-replacement-ordering-missing` | Engine combat replacement state-machine defect — source-side all-combat-damage prevention and a source-bound combat replacement can apply to the same player-damage packet, but the affected player receives no ordering decision. | Fixed; red `c84fce95`, green `4e80119c` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test combat_prevention_replacement_ordering_red -- --nocapture`. It ends invariant-valid and reconciles the catalog partition to 220 full / 36 partial / 35 catalog-only names (235 / 36 / 35 printings). The broader engine still does not expose arbitrary legal damage-allocation choices; it uses deterministic lethal-first assignment for this legal Trample execution. |

| `rav-bramble-manifest-count-stale-after-sagittars-merge` | RAV merge-integration defect — Bramble Elemental was promoted from a pre-Selesnya-Sagittars baseline, leaving the fixed positive manifest and coverage oracle one entry short. | Fixed; red `223208e9`, green `322a89ff` | The red command failed at compile time with 218 declared versus 219 initialized IDs; the correction reconciled the post-Sagittars partition before later Autochthon and Dimir promotions. |

| `bramble-elemental-controlled-aura-trigger-missing` | RAV Green/core trigger gap — Bramble Elemental had no observer for an Aura entering under its controller, so the optional typed Saproling trigger could not reach the stack. | Fixed; red `cbbe484c`, green `2f40fe22` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test bramble_elemental_red -- --nocapture`; it failed 0/1 at `Bramble Elemental controlled-Aura trigger binding exists`. The repair adds the expansion-neutral `ControlledAuraEntersBattlefield` observer condition, captured from a live Aura-like permanent and matched only to permanents with the same current controller. The focused trace is `AuraAttached → TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → TokenCreated → AbilityResolved` when the controller accepts; decline records no `TokenCreated`, and an opponent-controlled Aura queues no Bramble trigger. Each branch ends with `validate_invariants()` valid. The reconciled catalog partition is 219 full / 37 partial / 35 catalog-only unique names (234 / 37 / 35 printings). |

| `partial-damage-redirection-generic-decision-missing` | Engine replacement/state-machine defect — medium/high scope for every represented bounded redirection competing with prevention on a larger one-effect targeted damage spell. The generic decision surface admitted only redirects that could replace the whole current packet, silently routing partial redirect cases through the legacy deterministic path. | Fixed; red `b0ad74a4`, green `f8d3506f` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test partial_damage_redirection_generic_decision_red -- --nocapture`. It failed 0/1 at `partial redirect and shield require a replacement decision`: a 3-damage spell facing a 2-point redirect plus a 2-point shield exposed no `PendingDecision`. The repair introduces a typed deferred packet queue within `DecisionContinuation::DamageReplacement`. Selecting the redirect emits `DamageReplacementApplied → DamageRedirected(2)`, commits the new recipient packet, then re-evaluates the protected 1-damage remainder and consumes its shield before `DecisionCompleted → SpellResolved`. Every deferred packet has a positive amount, legal current target/incarnation, valid affected player, and no duplicate used identity; focused tests, the engine suite, and strict engine Clippy pass. Boundary: this remains a one-effect one-target direct-damage spell continuation; multi-instruction stack spells, optional replacement decisions, and a fully general replacement-event algebra remain separate work. |

| `selesnya-sagittars-combat-damage-activation-missing` | RAV card-fidelity gap — Selesnya Sagittars exposes Reach but omits its tap activation to damage an attacking or blocking creature, despite the expansion-neutral stack, target-shape, and resolution-time combat-target checks already existing. | Fixed; red `f0545080`, green `9587cff3` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test selesnya_sagittars_full_fidelity_red -- --nocapture`. It failed 0/2: the static definition lacked `tap-damage-attacking-or-blocking-creature`, then the behavior probe received `IllegalAction("source does not have the requested activated ability")`. The correction binds a zero-mana tap activation to the typed `AttackingOrBlockingCreature` slot and one normal `DealDamage` instruction. The green trace records `AttackersDeclared → AbilityActivated → DamageDealtToPermanent(1) → AbilityResolved`; the direct contract verifies the source tap, target damage, and final engine invariants. The shown corpus is 180 scenarios; catalog coverage is 218 full / 38 partial / 35 catalog-only unique names (233 / 38 / 35 printings). |

| `public-game-fields-can-bypass-transition-machine` | Engine API-encapsulation weakness — a caller can directly rewrite a public `PlayerState::life` field in a live game without a rule action or canonical receipt, yielding a shape-valid state that `validate_invariants` cannot distinguish from a legal transition. | Open; red regression pending | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test state_provenance_boundary_red -- --nocapture`. It fails 0/1 with `fabricated life transition: audit=Ok(()); life=13; events_unchanged=true`; the only receipts remain `StepBegan` for turn-one Untap and Upkeep. The test intentionally requires the audit to reject the receipt-free live life change. This is a provenance—not shape—gap: the public fixture API exposes `Game::players` and every `PlayerState` field, while `event_log_integrity` can only detect edits to the log itself. |

| `combat-mutual-lethal-block-history-provenance` | Core combat state-machine defect — after a legal blocker assignment, mutually lethal combat damage changed both combatants' zone identities; the stale assignment then failed the block-history audit and atomically rolled back the final priority pass. | Fixed; red `d61af4e2`, green `2d70ad47` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test blocker_history_mutual_lethal_red -- --nocapture`; it failed 0/1 with `left: Err("current blocker assignment lacks block-history provenance")`, `right: Ok(())`, after `BlockersDeclared { assignments: [(ObjectId(17), ObjectId(18))] }` and the first post-block `PriorityPassed`. The broader deterministic policy fuzz independently failed at seed `0xA11CE5E012345678`, operation 93. The narrow green repair removes ordinary battlefield departures from live combat membership before advancing their zone incarnation, retaining immutable exact-incarnation history only for delayed effects; the focused two-order fixture passes 2/2, with both `StateBasedAction { reason: "creature has lethal damage" }` receipts and both graveyard moves retained. The fuzz seed and full engine test package pass. |

| `golgari-brownscale-graveyard-to-hand-trigger-missing` | RAV/core trigger gap — Golgari Brownscale was bounded because the trigger scheduler could not observe an exact source transition from its graveyard to its controller's hand after a Dredge replacement. | Fixed; red `3c18eeb3`, green `52197108`, docs `0332be49` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test golgari_brownscale_graveyard_trigger_red -- --nocapture` failed at positive-manifest membership. | `golgari-thug-death-trigger-missing` | RAV/card-effect gap — Golgari Thug was bounded because a selected creature card from its controller's graveyard could not be moved to its owner's library top from a death trigger. | Fixed; red `a3a2d06c`, green `ff87b41a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test golgari_thug_death_trigger_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed at positive-manifest membership: `Golgari Thug cannot be positive-manifest while its death trigger is absent`. Its trace is `StateBasedAction(Thug) → CardMoved(Graveyard) → DecisionOpened(TriggeredAbilityTargets) → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CardMoved(chosen Watchwolf, Library) → AbilityResolved`; the Thug remains in the graveyard, the Watchwolf is the draw top above a Forest, and the final invariant audit passes. |
| `stinkweed-imp-combat-damage-trigger-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — Stinkweed Imp was bounded despite the engine already having exact combat-damage recipient trigger provenance and destruction. | Fixed; red `769d2842`, green `83ce42e8` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test stinkweed_imp_combat_trigger_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed at positive-manifest membership: `Stinkweed Imp cannot be positive-manifest while its combat-damage trigger is absent`. | `engine-stack-object-id-fixture-drift` | Engine test-fixture integration defect — `StackObject` gained a required monotonic `StackObjectId`, but several adversarial invariant fixtures still construct stack entries without an identity, preventing the engine test targets from compiling before their invariant intent can run. | Fixed; red `a105c071`, green `c19ac2a4` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --tests --no-run` from `varieties/magic`; it failed with `E0063: missing field \`id\` in initializer of \`StackObject\`` at `engine/tests/stack_unsupported_nonpermanent_integrity_red.rs:41`, `stack_resolution_atomicity_red.rs:62` and `:77`, and `stack_departed_controller_integrity_red.rs:41`. The fixture-only repair adds fixed ids and first advances the private allocator through an ordinary warmup cast, so the audited states are rejected for their intended reasons rather than identity drift. Focused traces verify the precise X-cost, departed-controller, sorcery-timing, self-target order, unsupported-card, and malformed-resolution errors; `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --tests --no-run` and strict `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass. No production code changed. |

 | `compulsive-research-private-conditional-discard-missing` | RAV Blue/core hidden-hand resolution gap — Compulsive Research is catalog-only because the engine has no target-recipient private post-draw choice that permits exactly one selected land discard or exactly two selected cards | Fixed; red `fafce451`, green `dd9bd11c` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test compulsive_research_red -- --nocapture`; it failed at `Compulsive Research definition exists`. The green repair adds the exact `{U}` creature Aura, an empty-change Aura attachment binding, and a target-free upkeep ability that returns only the currently attached permanent on the source's exact live incarnation. Focused evidence records `TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Hand) → AbilityResolved → StateBasedAction(Aura unattached) → CardMoved(Graveyard)`. The adversarial response trace destroys Mark with Seed Spark above that trigger; the old source resolves as a no-op, the former creature stays on the battlefield, and the final invariant audit passes. |
| `flight-of-fancy-not-reconciled` | RAV Blue card-coverage gap — Flight of Fancy had no executable definition, creature-Aura attachment, or stack-backed two-card ETB draw | Fixed; red `4f90fb2f`, green `3e00dca5` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test flight_of_fancy_red -- --nocapture`. It failed at `Flight of Fancy definition exists` before any state transition. The port uses the generic Aura attachment lifecycle with `Enchant creature` and a permanent Flying change, then stacks its own target-free ETB trigger. Focused event evidence is `SpellCast → SpellResolved → CardMoved(Battlefield) → ContinuousEffectCreated(Ability) → AuraAttached → TriggeredAbilityStacked → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Hand) ×2 → AbilityResolved`; both draws, the live attachment, and the post-resolution invariant audit are explicit. |
| `vedalken-entrancer-not-reconciled` | RAV Blue card-coverage gap — Vedalken Entrancer had no executable definition or `{U}, {T}` target-player mill-two activation | Fixed; red `4f90fb2f`, green `3e00dca5` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test vedalken_entrancer_red -- --nocapture`. It failed at `Vedalken Entrancer definition exists` before payment, target validation, stack placement, or a milling receipt. The port records the exact `{3}{U}` 1/4 Defender body and standard stack-backed `{U}, {T}` target-player mill-two activation. Focused evidence is `AbilityManaPaid({U}) → AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Graveyard) ×2 → AbilityResolved`; the source taps, the selected player alone mills, and the final invariant audit passes. |
| `untap-target-permanent-effect-missing` | Engine stack-effect gap — high severity for any card that must untap an arbitrary permanent rather than only a land or its own source | Fixed; red `cb27e48f`, green `c5feff9f` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test tidewater_minion_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile: `no variant, associated function, or constant named UntapTargetPermanent found for enum Effect`; the nearest existing effect permitted only a land. The green substrate adds the expansion-neutral `Effect::UntapTargetPermanent`, a typed `Permanent` target slot, resolution-time battlefield recheck, and no duplicate receipt for an already-untapped target. Tidewater Minion's trace is `AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → PermanentUntapped → AbilityResolved`, followed by `{U}` payment and a layer-six Defender removal effect. The invariant contract now distinguishes source, land, and arbitrary-permanent untap instructions while preserving one `PermanentUntapped` receipt per actual state change. |

| `departing-player-virtual-spell-copy-remains-on-stack` | Engine multiplayer/stack lifecycle defect — high severity, any virtual spell copy controlled by a player who loses in a continuing multiplayer game | Fixed; red `9c437154`, green `6dc81750` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test virtual_spell_copy_departure_red -- --nocapture`. The copy effect created virtual `ObjectId(3)` above player 0's physical instant. After player 0 reached zero life in a three-player game, SBA logged `PlayerLost` and `ObjectLeftGame` for physical objects but left `StackObject { card: ObjectId(3), controller: PlayerId(0), ... }` live. The regression failed `a player who left the game cannot retain a virtual spell copy on the stack` (0 passed, 1 failed). The repair removes every virtual copy controlled by the departing seat before physical-object cleanup, records `SpellCopyLeftGame { copy, original, controller }`, blocks event-log resets while a copy needs its `SpellCopied` provenance, and audits each copy as live or exactly once terminal. Green trace: `PlayerLost → SpellCopyLeftGame → ObjectLeftGame ×2 → next survivor's Upkeep`; the stack is empty and `validate_invariants()` passes. |
| `generalized-activation-selected-mana-composition-missing` | Engine activation-cost/state-machine gap — high severity, every represented generalized activated cost that also needs controller-selected generic or hybrid mana | Fixed; red `15c99530`, green `9669e6d3` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test generalized_activation_payment_selection_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile with `error[E0560]: struct GeneralizedAbilityActivation has no field named mana_payment_selection`, so the public generalized activation request could not carry the explicit generic/hybrid allocation already supported by ordinary bound activation. The green API adds one optional `ManaPaymentSelection` to the existing generalized activation request and delegates to the ordinary selected-spend payment path inside the same atomic transaction. It preserves selected colors as source-incarnation-bound calculated-cost provenance and on the live ability stack object; the invariant audit rejects malformed hybrid symbols, stale cost/activation identities, and a live stack allocation that disagrees with the recorded selection. Focused event evidence is `ActivatedAbilityCostCalculated(payment_selection=[hybrid Blue, generic Red]) → AbilityManaPaid({1}{U/R}) → AbilityLifePaid(2) → AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Hand) → AbilityResolved`; Blue spent on the hybrid symbol makes the conditional draw resolve. An unavailable second Blue selection rolls back mana, life, stack, and receipts atomically. |

| `event-log-reset-strands-live-decision` | Engine state-machine/provenance defect — high severity, every typed no-priority continuation exposed to scenario measurement | Fixed; red `2f027d43`, green `ecf87b8a` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test event_log_reset_pending_decision_red -- --nocapture`. A legal top-library reorder decision was opened, then the public `Game::clear_event_log()` helper erased `DecisionOpened` while leaving the pending continuation live. The invariant audit failed with `Err(IllegalAction("pending decision violates its identity, cardinality, or no-priority boundary"))` and an empty event log. The repair makes reset a receipt-quiescent boundary: it defers without mutating either log whenever clearing would orphan typed decision, activated/triggered stack-ability, permission-timed spell, delayed linked-exile, attached Equipment, or terminal provenance. Focused green trace retains `DecisionOpened(LibraryReorder)`, accepts the original exact decision id, and completes the spell under `validate_invariants()`. |
| `policy-spell-cost-selection-missing` | Engine policy-action / spell-cost gap — high severity, every represented chosen-X or spent-mana-conditional spell | Fixed; red `57e26408`, green `f86d5595` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test policy_cast_payment_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. It failed to compile because `PolicyAction::CastWithPayment` did not exist: a submitted policy had only legacy `Cast`, which routes to `Game::cast_spell` and cannot supply a chosen `{X}` or explicit generic/hybrid allocation. The green action carries one optional X plus one exact allocation and delegates to the existing atomic selected-spend/chosen-X paths, preserving `SpellManaPaid → SpellCast`, stack provenance, and `PolicyMoveKind::Cast`. Focused live-game evidence uses typed basic-land payment sources: the chosen-X trace spends three Black symbols, destroys the mana-value-two target, and rejects an incomplete allocation with no source tap, pool, zone, stack, event-log, or accepted-policy-move mutation; the spent-mana trace pays Black plus Blue and draws exactly once. Audit boundary: RAV has no Phyrexian-mana cost in this set; its represented hybrid, Convoke, creature-sacrifice additional cost, X, and spent-mana conditional forms now have typed cast surfaces. Alternative/rather-than, player-counter, hidden-zone, and generalized nonmana spell costs remain separate unsupported cost families. |
| `damage-replacement-generic-decision-unification-missing` | Engine replacement/state-machine defect — high severity, affected-player choice across prevention/redirection and quantity replacement families | Fixed; red `5cdda9e6`, green `4069a793` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test damage_replacement_generic_decision_red -- --nocapture`. It failed at `damage replacements use the generic decision surface`: competing prevention and redirection opened only the legacy projection, while token/counter replacements used `PendingDecision`. The repair adds a typed `DecisionContinuation::DamageReplacement` with captured spell-source and target incarnations, `ReplacementChoice::Damage` options, and the same stale-safe `DecisionId` submission path. The legacy `ChooseDamageReplacement` action now validates its old fields then delegates to the current generic id. Focused event evidence is `DecisionOpened(Replacement) → DamageReplacementApplied(Redirect) → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer → DecisionCompleted(Replacement) → SpellResolved`; prevention has the analogous `DamagePrevented` batch. Boundary: the unified decision representation remains limited to one targeted direct-damage spell, full-event redirects, and the existing shield/protection kinds; partial redirects, optional replacements, and a fully generalized replacement-event algebra remain open. |

| `replacement-chain-affected-player-ordering-missing` | Engine replacement/state-machine defect — high severity, concurrent token/counter multipliers and future prevention/redirection chains | Fixed; red `779f9189`, green `78a1ac5a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test replacement_chain_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it initially failed to compile because `ReplacementChoice`, `DecisionKind::Replacement`, `DecisionSelection::Replacements`, and `PendingDecisionView.replacement_candidates` did not exist. The green substrate reuses the id-bearing `PendingDecision` boundary for one-effect token creation and counter placement. It captures source incarnations in every quantity choice and receipt, applies one affected-player-selected option, recomputes live candidates, opens a fresh decision only while two or more remain, automatically applies one remaining candidate, and commits the ordinary token/counter event only after the finite chain ends. Focused trace: `DecisionOpened(1) → ReplacementEffectApplied(1→3) → DecisionCompleted(1) → DecisionOpened(2) → ReplacementEffectApplied(3→6) → DecisionCompleted(2) → ReplacementEffectApplied(6→24) → TokenCreated ×24 → SpellResolved`; the counter path shares that chain and reaches `CounterPlaced(6)`. Existing two-Doubling-Season coverage now submits the public order explicitly. The bounded direct-damage path now shares the same typed decision surface through `ReplacementChoice::Damage`; partial replacement, optional replacements, non-quantity token/counter transformations, and a fully generalized replacement-event algebra remain open. |

| `combat-damage-order-and-deathtouch-missing` | Engine combat/state-machine defect — high severity, every multi-block attacker and every deathtouch source | Fixed; red `3ebb4ef5`, green `8c3b8a70` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test combat_damage_order_deathtouch_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile because neither `Keyword::Deathtouch` nor `DecisionKind::CombatDamageOrder` existed. The repair migrates CR 509.2 multi-block ordering to the public generic `PendingDecision` slot: after `BlockersDeclared`, the attacking player must submit one complete blocker permutation per multi-block group before priority; `DecisionOpened → DecisionCompleted → CombatDamageOrderChosen` preserves replay-visible order. Stale, partial, duplicate, foreign, and out-of-group answers remain atomic through the existing id/cardinality boundary. It also records committed deathtouch-damage provenance on the target incarnation, assigns one positive lethal point per blocker, applies the lethal SBA independently of toughness, and clears that provenance at regeneration, cleanup, and battlefield re-entry. Focused trace proves a 7/7 deathtouch trampler ordered against 4/4 then 2/2 blockers deals `1 + 1` and `5` to the defender, after which both blockers reach their ordinary graveyard SBA transition. Boundary: policies still cannot choose arbitrary legal damage amounts, combat prevention/replacement ordering is not yet generalized, and attack/block constraints remain bounded. |

| `control-change-derived-controller-substrate-missing` | Engine continuous-effect / object-control gap — high severity, blocks control-changing cards and corrupts controller-relative rules when approximated | Fixed; red `c2c74d8d`, green `86f0acd4` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test control_change_red -- --nocapture`, which failed to compile because `Effect::GainControlTargetUntilEndOfTurn`, `ContinuousChange::ChangeController`, `Game::controller_of`, and `GameEvent::ControllerChanged` did not exist. The green repair adds owner-indexed battlefield vectors, layer-two timestamped derived control, live `controller_of` projection, public controller-change receipts, cleanup/source-departure reversion, controller-relative target/Aura/SBA/view/untap/combat/trigger/cost/replacement integration, and controller-change summoning-sickness provenance. The focused synthetic contract is 6/6: a stolen permanent appears only in its new controller's view; a temporary spell records `ContinuousEffectCreated → ControllerChanged` and reverses at cleanup; a persistent source departure reverses; a controlled permanent returns to its owner's hand; an Aura whose `ControlledCreature` target ceases to be controlled is SBA-cleaned; and a creature stolen this turn cannot attack. Boundary: only the target-permanent `GainControlTargetUntilEndOfTurn` stack effect is represented so far. Permanent control, exchange, controller-chooses, control-changing Auras, and full layer-2 dependency/copy interactions remain separate core work. |

This is a public discovery ledger for the Rust RAV engine slice. It deliberately
separates engine defects from policy/harness outcomes. Each open engine item is
reproduced by `cargo run -p cardbench-magic-policies --bin rav-engine-audit`
from `varieties/magic`; the audit exits nonzero while an item remains open.

| `trigger-event-recipient-and-opponent-graveyard-provenance-missing` | Engine trigger-scheduler gap — high severity, combat "that creature" effects and card-to-opponent-graveyard triggers | Fixed; red `6efe9093`, green `f6643f10` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test trigger_breadth_red -- --nocapture`. Both synthetic cases failed: combat damage opened an arbitrary `DistinctCreature` target choice rather than retaining its recipient, and a hand-to-opponent-graveyard discard left the observer at `Some(1)` rather than `Some(2)`. The green substrate adds `DealsCombatDamageToCreature` with an exact-recipient/incarnation payload and non-targeting materialized destruction, plus `OpponentCardPutIntoGraveyard` driven by every ordinary graveyard zone transition. Focused trace: `DamageDealtToPermanent(source, recipient) → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CardDestroyed(recipient)`, with no target decision; and `CardDiscarded → CardMoved(Graveyard) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CounterPlaced`. Binding and live-stack invariants reject escaped runtime provenance effects and ensure a captured recipient cannot affect a later incarnation. Boundary: this does not yet add complete APNAP policy ordering, intervening-if predicates, or the broader full trigger scheduler. |

| `composable-activated-life-counter-return-x-costs-missing` | Engine activation-cost/state-machine gap — high severity, abilities with nonmana payments beyond existing discard/sacrifice/tap fields | Fixed; red `035941c8`, green `783ae78a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test generalized_activated_costs_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Before the repair it failed to compile because there was no typed generalized cost profile/binding, policy request, activation API, or life/counter/return/X receipts. The green substrate binds immutable profiles to existing ability ids before a game begins, accepts exact policy-selected counter and permanent objects as one priority action, preflights full mana including `{X}`, life, counter totals, and return selections, then commits all receipts before `AbilityActivated`. Focused event evidence: `AbilityManaPaid({4}) → AbilityLifePaid(2) → CounterRemovedAsAbilityCost(charge,2) → CounterRemoved(charge,2) → ReturnedAsAbilityCost → CardMoved(Hand) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → AbilityXCostChosen(3) → AbilityActivated`. The companion adversarial contract selects unaffordable `{X}=4` and proves mana, life, counters, zones, stack, and event log roll back atomically. Boundary: this profile API does not yet compose explicit generic/hybrid mana-color selection with generalized costs, nor does it model alternative/rather-than/conditional-resolution costs, player counters, or costs that choose hidden-zone objects. |

| `copy-layer-copiable-values-missing` | Engine object-characteristics / lifecycle gap — high severity, all permanent and token copy effects | Fixed; red `396f8cd3`, green `01168568` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test copy_semantics_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed to compile because `Game::copy_permanent` and the required `PermanentCopied`/`PermanentCopyExpired` receipts did not exist. The green substrate adds an object-local layer-one `CopiableValues` snapshot distinct from derived `Characteristics`: it copies a card definition or token specification, never counters, marked damage, or later-layer effects. It is timestamped and target-incarnation-bound, survives source departure, and expires on target departure. Effective definition lookup safely grants copied definition-bound abilities/static bindings; `AbilityActivated` retains its effective definition so the historical event remains valid after the target resumes its printed identity. The focused trace is `PermanentCopied(source=1,target=2) → AbilityActivated(definition=TST-COPY-SOURCE) → DamageDealtToPlayer → CardDestroyed(source) → CardDestroyed(target) → PermanentCopyExpired(target)`. It also proves a card can copy a Spirit token's values without becoming a token. Boundary: copy effects are currently installed through the explicit public `Game::copy_permanent` transition; spell-copy/casting-permission effects, replacement-of-copy choices, and full copy-effect duration/exception rules remain separate substrates. |
| `unified-decision-identity-and-continuation-missing` | Engine policy-decision state-machine defect — critical severity, every pending public or hidden selection | Fixed; red `b5ffdc1c`, green `0ed11f53`/`a67ef064` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test unified_decision_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` printed `stale unified-decision probe: result=Ok(())` and then `SacrificedByEffect { source: ObjectId(1), player: PlayerId(0), permanent: ObjectId(3) }`: a source/ability/selection action from an earlier upkeep was indistinguishable from a later identical prompt. The repair provides one cloned typed `PendingDecision` slot with a monotonic nonreusable `DecisionId`, typed visibility/options/cardinality/continuation, `PolicyAction::SubmitDecision`, and public `DecisionOpened`/`DecisionCompleted` receipts with no hidden candidates. A stale id now reports `Err(IllegalAction("stale or unknown decision id"))` without changing the view or receipt log; wrong-player, illegal-option, duplicate, min/max, and priority submissions likewise roll back atomically. The first real migrations are public triggered sacrifice plus private triggered discard and library-search continuations. Chord proves an opponent sees neither the generic pending decision nor library candidates, while final `LibrarySearchResolved`, `CardDiscarded`, and `SacrificedByEffect` receipts remain specialized. Focused unified-decision, Chord, Sadistic Augermage, and Vindictive Mob contracts plus strict engine-library Clippy pass. Boundary: targets, optional costs, colors, trigger/replacement ordering, and combat assignment have not yet moved to the generic slot; legacy specialized actions remain compatibility shims. |

| `linked-exile-delayed-return-substrate-missing` | Engine object-identity / delayed-action gap — critical for Aura blink effects and any later linked exile | Fixed; red `e61c160e`/`610e8572`, green `d935d778`/`d6201faf` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test linked_exile_delayed_return_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed `left: Some(Graveyard)`, `right: Some(Exile)`: the existing creature-exile instruction orphaned the attached Aura. The repair adds typed, clonable `LinkedExileGroup`/`DelayedAction` state, exact exile-incarnation members, deterministic creature-before-Aura return, and schedule/consume receipts. The green trace records creature `ObjectId(1)` exile incarnation 2 and Aura `ObjectId(2)` exile incarnation 4, then `DelayedActionScheduled`, a lower stale-target spell `SpellCounteredByRules`, and at end step creature incarnation 3 before Aura incarnation 5 / `AuraAttached` / `DelayedActionConsumed`. A member that no longer remains in that exact exile incarnation is skipped; no stable ID alone can revive or reattach it. Focused green regression and strict engine-library Clippy pass. Boundary: this substrate does not yet promote Flickerform or model policy ordering for simultaneous delayed actions. |

## Discovery batch 2026-07-30

| ID | Classification | Status | Reproducible observation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `damage-cannot-be-prevented-incorrectly-bypasses-nonprevention-replacements` | Engine prospective-event/replacement defect — high severity, every unpreventable damage source interacting with redirection | Fixed; red `c2459d19`, green `c6476e92` | The engine's former narrow replacement path treated `Keyword::DamageCannotBePrevented` as an override of both prevention and redirection. That is too broad: the rule excludes prevention only, while a Razia-style damage-redirection replacement remains applicable. Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test damage_cannot_be_prevented_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Before the fix it reported `left: 2, right: 0` for the protected permanent and emitted no `DamageRedirected` receipt. The correction retains redirection but bypasses the target shield: the focused green trace is `DamageShieldCreated → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer`, with no `DamagePrevented`. It also adds a bounded prospective-event choice pipeline: two live effects suspend one targeted direct-damage spell, the affected player submits an exact replacement identity, and `DamageReplacementApplied` precedes `DamagePrevented` or `DamageRedirected`. |
| `incarnation-receipt-breaks-library-search-audit` | Engine invariant-integration defect — high severity, every library search that finds a card | Fixed; red `8849581d`, green `170fc343` | After incarnation receipts were enabled, Civic Wayfinder moved its chosen library card, emitted `ObjectIncarnationAdvanced`, then emitted `LibrarySearchResolved`. The search audit required `CardMoved` immediately before the search receipt and rejected the valid action with `library-search receipt lacks movement to its declared destination`. Exact red command: `cargo run -q -p cardbench-magic-rav --bin rav-engine-parity`; it failed at `rav_civic_wayfinder_etb_basic_land_search action 4`. The audit now accepts exactly one matching structural incarnation receipt between its selected-card move and resolution receipt while preserving the immediate post-resolution shuffle requirement; `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test civic_wayfinder_search_red --quiet` passes. |
| `incarnation-receipt-breaks-activated-sacrifice-audit` | Engine invariant-integration defect — high severity, self-sacrifice and other activated sacrifice costs | Fixed; red `49bc3953`, green `f12fc79c` | After object-incarnation receipts and calculated activation costs were combined, a legal self-sacrifice activation wrote `SacrificedAsAbilityCost → CardMoved(Graveyard) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → AbilityActivated`. The sacrifice audit expected `CardMoved` immediately before `AbilityActivated`, stopped at the structural incarnation receipt, and rejected the otherwise valid transition with `IllegalAction("sacrifice-cost receipts do not match the activated ability binding")`. Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test activated_cost_modification_red tax_does_not_apply_to_mana_abilities_and_departure_revokes_it -- --exact --nocapture`. The invariant now steps over an incarnation receipt only when it is paired with its immediately preceding move, then consumes the original cost receipt and transition as one causal unit. The focused test passes and prints the complete taxed self-sacrifice lifecycle. |
| `general-named-counter-permanent-lifecycle-gap` | Engine counter-state defect — high severity, expansion-neutral permanent counters and later counter-removal effects | Fixed; red `0036b14c`, green `020beaba` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test general_counter_lifecycle_red -- --nocapture` failed with `left: None`, `right: Some(1)` after `TST-COUNTER-ENGINE` resolved its counter ability: the artifact had `counters: {}` and no `CounterPlaced` receipt. The green substrate introduces typed `CounterKind`, `CounterPlaced`/`CounterRemoved` receipts, typed add/remove source and permanent-target effects, positive/valid-kind and battlefield lifecycle invariants, atomic insufficient-removal rejection, and exact typed replacement-chain auditing. `general_counter_lifecycle_contract` prints `ReplacementEffectApplied(CounterPlacement { Charge }, 2→4) → CounterPlaced(Charge, 4)` and a later `CounterRemoved(Charge, 3)`; an attempted second removal reports `IllegalAction("counter removal requires sufficient counters")` while preserving the one remaining counter, live stack, and pre-resolution event log. Normal departure clears a named `quest` counter. Boundary: player/nonbattlefield counters and counter-removal activation costs remain unsupported. |
| `object-incarnation-zone-transition-and-source-provenance-gap` | Engine zone-change identity defect — critical severity, stack, source-relative effects, direct draw, and any later blink/linked-exile substrate | Fixed; red `784ab48d`, green `d09e0b0c` | Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test object_incarnation_zone_change_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed with `hand=1; stack=1; library=1; hand_after_draw=1` (the expected new incarnations were `2`) and later with `original=1; returned=3; characteristics power Some(2)`. The fix advances every real zone transition, including stack entry and draws, records `ObjectIncarnationAdvanced` immediately after the corresponding `CardMoved` or `SpellCast`, captures stack/effect/Aura endpoint incarnations, and makes source-relative effects no-op for an old source. It also keys activated/triggered lifecycle receipts by source incarnation; the green regression proves a dies trigger stacks and resolves with its old battlefield incarnation. Focused proof: the red test is now 3/3, `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --quiet` passes, and `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-engine --lib -- -D warnings` passes. RAV test sources compile; public scenario digests need their separate expected-event migration because the canonical stream now includes the new structural receipt. |
| `generic-trigger-scheduler-condition-decision-gap` | Engine trigger-scheduling defect — high severity, every non-attack trigger with targets, a targetless “may” decision, or a public discard/sacrifice selection | Fixed; red `41e4dedb`, green `163229ec` | The former card-shaped damage, dies, life-gain, ETB, land-entry, upkeep, attack, and cast queues now capture one `PendingTriggeredAbilityEvent` pipeline, materialize event-bound effects before placement, and place controller groups active-player-first. A target-bearing event remains off-stack until the existing controller target action succeeds; subsequent placements cannot overtake that choice. Optional triggers now suspend for an explicit accept/decline even with zero mana cost. One-effect all-player discard and controller-creature sacrifice triggers suspend at resolution for the actual chooser's `ChooseTriggeredAbilityEffectObject` submission, so neither selection uses stable fixture order. Exact green proof: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test belltower_sphinx_trigger_scheduler_red -- --nocapture` (2/2) prints `DamageDealtToPermanent → TriggeredAbilityStacked → ChooseTriggeredAbilityTargets → CardMoved(Graveyard) → AbilityResolved`; its target decision is controller-only and the stack is empty before submission. The Sadistic Augermage and Vindictive Mob contracts additionally prove each player chooses its own discard and the controller chooses the sacrificed creature. Remaining scope: controller ordering among multiple simultaneous triggers is stable binding/source order; a later policy ordering decision is still required for full CR 603.3b fidelity. |
| `apnap-simultaneous-trigger-ordering-missing` | Engine trigger-scheduler defect — high severity, every represented simultaneous group with multiple triggers controlled by one or more players | Fixed; red `2240fcbb`, green `4f92f529` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test apnap_trigger_ordering_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it failed because two simultaneous same-controller triggers entered the stack in fixture order with no decision. The green slice adds public `TriggeredAbilityOrder` decisions with exact source/incarnation/ability options and full-permutation validation, then preserves existing active-player-first APNAP grouping. Event evidence: a land entry opens Player 0's `DecisionOpened(TriggeredAbilityOrder)`, then `DecisionCompleted → TriggeredAbilityOrderChosen([second, first]) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(second) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(first) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(nonactive)`. The no-priority boundary and receipt ordering are invariant-audited. Boundary: not every Oracle trigger condition or intervening-if predicate is represented yet; this orders the engine's existing simultaneous trigger batches. |
| `generic-mana-spend-provenance-missing` | Engine payment/stack-provenance defect — high severity, every conditional-on-spent-mana spell | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `ManaPool::pay` deterministically drained generic mana by color order and left no player-selected allocation, stack provenance, or event receipt.
